13a:["$","ul",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["\n",["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"-R"}],", ",["$","code",null,{"children":"--repository <REPOSITORY>"}]," — Path to repository to operate on"]}],"\n",["$","p",null,{"children":"By default, Jujutsu searches for the closest .jj/ directory in an ancestor of the current working directory."}],"\n"]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["\n",["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"--ignore-working-copy"}]," — Don't snapshot the working copy, and don't update it"]}],"\n",["$","p",null,{"children":["By default, Jujutsu snapshots the working copy at the beginning of every command. The working copy is also updated at the end of the command, if the command modified the working-copy commit (",["$","code",null,{"children":"@"}],"). If you want to avoid snapshotting the working copy and instead see a possibly stale working-copy commit, you can use ",["$","code",null,{"children":"--ignore-working-copy"}],". This may be useful e.g. in a command prompt, especially if you have another process that commits the working copy."]}],"\n",["$","p",null,{"children":["Loading the repository at a specific operation with ",["$","code",null,{"children":"--at-operation"}]," implies ",["$","code",null,{"children":"--ignore-working-copy"}],"."]}],"\n"]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["\n",["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"--ignore-immutable"}]," — Allow rewriting immutable commits"]}],"\n",["$","p",null,{"children":"By default, Jujutsu prevents rewriting commits in the configured set of immutable commits. This option disables that check and lets you rewrite any commit but the root commit."}],"\n",["$","p",null,{"children":["This option only affects the check. It does not affect the ",["$","code",null,{"children":"immutable_heads()"}]," revset or the ",["$","code",null,{"children":"immutable"}]," template keyword."]}],"\n"]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["\n",["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"--at-operation <AT_OPERATION>"}]," [alias: ",["$","code",null,{"children":"at-op"}],"] — Operation to load the repo at"]}],"\n",["$","p",null,{"children":"Operation to load the repo at. By default, Jujutsu loads the repo at the most recent operation, or at the merge of the divergent operations if any."}],"\n",["$","p",null,{"children":["You can use ",["$","code",null,{"children":"--at-op=<operation ID>"}]," to see what the repo looked like at an earlier operation. For example ",["$","code",null,{"children":"jj --at-op=<operation ID> st"}]," will show you what ",["$","code",null,{"children":"jj st"}]," would have shown you when the given operation had just finished. ",["$","code",null,{"children":"--at-op=@"}]," is pretty much the same as the default except that divergent operations will never be merged."]}],"\n",["$","p",null,{"children":["Use ",["$","code",null,{"children":"jj op log"}]," to find the operation ID you want. Any unambiguous prefix of the operation ID is enough."]}],"\n",["$","p",null,{"children":["When loading the repo at an earlier operation, the working copy will be ignored, as if ",["$","code",null,{"children":"--ignore-working-copy"}]," had been specified."]}],"\n",["$","p",null,{"children":"It is possible to run mutating commands when loading the repo at an earlier operation. Doing that is equivalent to having run concurrent commands starting at the earlier operation. There's rarely a reason to do that, but it is possible."}],"\n"]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["\n",["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"--debug"}]," — Enable debug logging"]}],"\n"]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["\n",["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"--color <WHEN>"}]," — When to colorize output"]}],"\n",["$","p",null,{"children":["Possible values: ",["$","code",null,{"children":"always"}],", ",["$","code",null,{"children":"never"}],", ",["$","code",null,{"children":"debug"}],", ",["$","code",null,{"children":"auto"}]]}],"\n"]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["\n",["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"--quiet"}]," — Silence non-primary command output"]}],"\n","$L44f","\n","$L450","\n"]}],"\n","$L451","\n","$L452","\n","$L453","\n"]}]

145:["$","p",null,{"children":"Move changes from a revision into the stack of mutable revisions"}]
146:["$","p",null,{"children":"This command splits changes in the source revision and moves each change to the closest mutable ancestor where the corresponding lines were modified last. If the destination revision cannot be determined unambiguously, the change will be left in the source revision."}]

253:["$","p",null,{"children":["The primary use case for this command is to apply the results of automatic\ncode formatting tools to revisions that may not be properly formatted yet.\nIt can also be used to modify files with other tools like ",["$","code",null,{"children":"sed"}]," or ",["$","code",null,{"children":"sort"}],"."]}]
254:["$","p",null,{"children":"The changed files in the given revisions will be updated with any fixes\ndetermined by passing their file content through any external tools the user\nhas configured for those files. Descendants will also be updated by passing\ntheir versions of the same files through the same tools, which will ensure\nthat the fixes are not lost. This will never result in new conflicts. Files\nwith existing conflicts will be updated on all sides of the conflict, which\ncan potentially increase or decrease the number of conflict markers."}]
255:["$","p",null,{"children":"The external tools must accept the current file content on standard input,\nand return the updated file content on standard output. A tool's output will\nnot be used unless it exits with a successful exit code. Output on standard\nerror will be passed through to the terminal."}]
256:["$","p",null,{"children":"Tools are defined in a table where the keys are arbitrary identifiers and\nthe values have the following properties:"}]
257:["$","ul",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"command"}],": The arguments used to run the tool. The first argument is the\npath to an executable file. Arguments can contain these variables that\nwill be replaced:","\n",["$","ul",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"$$root"}]," will be replaced with the workspace root path (the directory\ncontaining the .jj directory)."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"$$path"}]," will be replaced with the repo-relative path of the file\nbeing fixed. It is useful to provide the path to tools that include\nthe path in error messages, or behave differently based on the\ndirectory or file name."]}],"\n"]}],"\n"]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"patterns"}],": Determines which files the tool will affect. If this list is\nempty, no files will be affected by the tool. If there are multiple\npatterns, the tool is applied only once to each file in the union of the\npatterns."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"enabled"}],": Enables or disables the tool. If omitted, the tool is enabled.\nThis is useful for defining disabled tools in user configuration that can\nbe enabled in individual repositories with one config setting."]}],"\n"]}]

316:["$","p",null,{"children":"The command effectively says \"these revisions are actually independent\",\nmeaning that they should no longer be ancestors/descendants of each other.\nHowever, revisions outside the set that were previously ancestors of a\nrevision in the set will remain ancestors of it. For example, revision 0\nabove remains an ancestor of both 1 and 2. Similarly,\nrevisions outside the set that were previously descendants of a revision\nin the set will remain descendants of it. For example, revision 3 above\nremains a descendant of both 1 and 2."}]

32a:["$","p",null,{"children":"There are three different ways of specifying which revisions to rebase:"}]
32b:["$","ul",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"--source/-s"}]," to rebase a revision and its descendants"]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"--branch/-b"}]," to rebase a whole branch, relative to the destination"]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"--revisions/-r"}]," to rebase the specified revisions without their\ndescendants"]}],"\n"]}]

32e:["$","ul",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"--destination/-d"}]," to rebase the revisions onto the specified targets"]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"--insert-after/-A"}]," to rebase the revisions onto the specified targets and\nto rebase the targets' descendants onto the rebased revisions"]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"--insert-before/-B"}]," to rebase the revisions onto the specified targets'\nparents and to rebase the targets and their descendants onto the rebased\nrevisions"]}],"\n"]}]

33d:["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"jj rebase -s X"}]," is similar to ",["$","code",null,{"children":"jj rebase -r X::"}]," and will behave the same\nif X is a single revision.
